# run9 英語レビュー: ワールド19・20（レッスン 541〜600）

- 日付: 2026-11-27
- やりかた: docs/run9/BRIEF.md の「Review method」。lint → 3人の レビュアー（A=アメリカの 小学校の 先生 / B=こどもの ESL・言語習得の 専門家 / C=台本を 読む 声優）を べつべつに 書いて から 決める。2人 いじょうが 指摘 or 客観的な まちがい（objective）だけ なおす。10 レッスンずつ 6 グループで レビュー
- lint（`npm run english:lint -- --from 541 --to 600`）: **まえ error 0 / warn 160 / info 14 → あと error 0 / warn 1 / info 14**（まえの warn: Okay・okay 146・autumn 5・ながい 文 5・タイトルと スライドの「…」3・ゆれ 1。あとの warn 1: 「thank you」〈L554 どうし〉と「thank-you」〈L569 a thank-you note の 名詞〉。どちらも ただしい つかいわけ なので のこす）
- なおした 欄: **720**（パッチ 776 件。機械的な なおし 13・ブロックの きめごと 480〈473 欄〉・レビュアー 278・あとの そろえ 5）。なおした レッスン: **60 / 60**
- 60 レッスン ぜんぶに `"reviewed": "run9"` を つけた
- パッチは ぜんぶ「before が いまの 値と おなじ ときだけ あてる」で あてた（はずれ 0）。60 レッスン ぜんぶで スライドの 検証（unknownSlideItems）・台本の 長さ（180〜240 秒）・英語の わりあいを たしかめた（ALL OK）。かいわ あいての チェック（.run9-w1920/partner.ts）も ALL OK

## 共通の きめごと（ブロック全体）
1. 日本語の なかの 英語の あとの 2重の 記号（「What should we do?。」）→ 英語がわの 記号だけ のこす（W01-18 と おなじ）
2. 話しことばの 2択「Yes, it is. Or, No, it isn't.」→「Yes, it is. Or no, it isn't.」、英語字幕は「A / B」（W03-18 と おなじ）。ただし L575 の「Use two hands. Or, Put it in the mailbox.」は ひとつづきの 指示なので、字幕も「Or put it in the mailbox.」（mechfix.mjs）
3. 「Okay / okay」→「OK」（159 か所。teacherScript・会話・スライド・L573・L598 の 学校の 表現も）
4. 文の なかの「Now, Touch ～」「Then, Fold ～」など → 小文字（218 欄。say・英語字幕・teacherScript。TPR の 項目と スライドは 大文字の まま。名前と I'm は そのまま）。ことばの ならびを おしえる ところ（First, next, then, finally.）は グループが 小文字に そろえた
5. イギリスふうの ほめことば: L576「Lovely work!」→「Great work!」、L583「Lovely manners, everyone!」→「Great manners, everyone!」、L587・L593「Brilliant!」→「Fantastic!」（グループで L543 の 小文字の brilliant、L596 の Lovely! も）。L581 の ことば「lovely」・「What a lovely house!」は 学習項目で、おきゃくさんの ことばと して アメリカでも しぜんなので のこした
6. エマ先生 じぶんの 語りの「Ms. Emma asks, / says,」→「I ask, / I say,」、日本語「エマ先生「」→「わたし「」（L592・L593・L595・L598・L599・L600。W17-18 と おなじ。こどもが「エマ先生」と よぶ 会話の 行は そのまま）
7. **AI かいわの さいしょの ひとこと「Hi! I'm Ms. Emma.」→ ほんとうの かいわ あいて（60 レッスン ぜんぶ）**。アプリは エマ先生を かいわ あいてに えらばない（pickConversationPartner）。さいごの「Next, talk with X!」の X に した（W17-18 と おなじ）。そのあと グループが「こどもが いうと へんな ところ」を なおした（L551・L554 は「Let's pretend I'm ...」、L552「My little brother」→「A little boy」、L599 は おまつりの まえの日 なのに「コンサートに いく」→「Tomorrow is the big day! I can't wait for the festival!」）
8. autumn → fall（L578・L580。W11-12 と おなじ）
9. 英語の タイトル・スライドの「…」→「...」（L573「I'd Love To, But...」・L589「Why Don't We...?」。W01-02 の きまり。titles.json と テストも なおした）
10. レッスンの はじめの「Yesterday we ～」→「Last time, we ～」（L562・L572。W13-16 と おなじ。グループが のこりの teacherScript と 日本語「きのうは」→「このまえは」も なおした）
11. 日本語の 過去形の よびかた「むかしの かたち」→「すぎた かたち」（L592。W15-18 と おなじ）
12. あとの そろえ（グループの 報告から）:
    - TURE の 音「cher」→「chur」（L571〜580 で 23 か所、L597・L599・L600 も。TTS が 名前の Cher と よむ。W17 と おなじ）
    - 「And now, Smile!」「OK, Play」「And, Turn」など、Now / Then いがいの ことばの あとの 大文字（グループで なおし、L556「And, You shouldn't ...」→「And you shouldn't ...」は あとで なおした）
    - L590「You learned all of these this week!」→「You learned all of these!」（「こんしゅう」は まいしゅう おなじ ペースで まなぶ ことが まえ。日本語も「もう ならったね」）
    - L588 の 強く よむ ところ「Cel-e-BRA-tion」→「Cel-e-BRAY-tion」（BRA を ことばと して よむ）。「Cel」を scripts/english-words.txt の 音節の ところに たした

## 学習項目 ID が かわった もの（記録が あたらしく なる）
- L573 表現「That's okay. Maybe next time!」→「That's OK. Maybe next time!」
- L598 表現「It's okay to be nervous.」→「It's OK to be nervous.」
- （タイトルの「…」→「...」は ID に かんけい ない。tests/curriculum.test.ts の L573 タイトル・L573/L598 の 表現・「It's OK to say no」を なおした）

## のこした こと（1人だけの 指摘・ほかの ワールドにも ある・あとで 決める）
- 「thank you / thank-you」の lint warn 1（どうしと 名詞の ただしい つかいわけ）
- 「Wind it up.」（L548 TPR・表現、L549 会話）: TTS が 風の wind と よむ かも。学習項目なので のこした（台本の「Wind, rewind.」は なおした）
- 音の つづり「kw」「skw」「sul」（W19）と「J, j, j!」「Ch, ch, ch!」（L586・L587）、「D G E says j」: TTS が 文字の 名前で よむ かも。ブロックで そろって いるので のこした（「juh」を たして まとめて かえる ほうほうが ある）
- L595 TPR「Throw your trash in the bin.」（アメリカでは trash can。学習項目なので のこした）
- L597・L600 の 日本語「むかし」（last year / the past の いみ。そろって いるので のこした。まえ・きょねん に する ほうほうが ある）
- phonics.tipJa の「ステージ2 の つづり①:」「まとめ:」「ふりかえり:」（W17-18 と おなじく のこした）
- AI かいわの「Please tell me ...」「Please sit down.」（こどもの ていねいな いいかたと して しぜん。lint info）
- 「Well done!」、「Very, very」（lint info）、「Thumbs up!」（かんたんの ことば）
- docs/curriculum-6year.md と exports/teacher の L573・L589 タイトルは「…」の まま（計画書と 書き出しは この ブロックの そとで、つぎに 書き出す ときに かわる）

## レビュアーの メモ（グループごと・英語）

### Run 9 review notes: L541-550 (world 19, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, "Yes, it is. Or no, it isn't." says, OK, US praise, "I say", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First, / After that, / Finally,", AI starters "Hi! I'm <partner>."). There are 69 patches in 10 lessons, so every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no changes to school.expressions[].en, vocab[].en, tpr[].en or phonics words. The only learning-item field I touched is the Japanese of L541 tpr[3]. The English in this world is clean and the fixes are small. Themes: (1) **the block-wide lowercase rule went too far**. After "First," it also lowercased the quoted order words Next / Then / Finally, so "First, next, Then, finally." now appears in mixed case (L547, L548, L550). (2) TTS problems: "Wind, rewind" (TTS reads bare "wind" as the air word), "El, e, va, tor", "Ree, again!" and "Ree, one more time" (both sound like "say ree again"). (3) Wording or facts a teacher would not use: "These are scissors... It's used for", a smoothie in a mixer, "You can't throw flat paper", "cut the corners" for a pinwheel slit, "Shall we help?", "What do we do finally?". (4) One lowercase "brilliant" that the British-praise fix missed. Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer, and the one over-12 line (the L543 starter) is split. The check script passes: ALL OK. The lessons are 218-227 s long with 97-98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L541 Tick, Tock, Click!
- A: The arrival at the Gadget Workshop is natural ("Here's the clink, clank sound!", "Look at all the machines."). The Inventor's note "Please use my tools..." is fine in a written note (lint info only, kept). "It washes socks!" is a fun example.
- B: makes with -s for one machine is clear, and the "make toys" quiz distractor is good. "re means again" is clear. The JA for tpr[3] "Turn the big wheel." is おおきな くるまを まわして. To a child くるま means "car", but the action line already says ハンドル.
- C: Reads well. "Creeeak!" and "Tick, tock, perfect!" are fun to say.
- Decision: tpr[3].ja, seg12 subtitleJa and seg13 subtitleJa: くるま -> ハンドル (objective JA). The English is unchanged.

#### L542 What's This Tool?
- A: The tool-shelf scene and the safety line (Later, Birimo. First, let's learn the safety rules.) are natural. In the teacherScript, "These are scissors. What are they used for? It's used for cutting paper." switches from plural to singular. "It's used for sticking things." sounds unfinished (a teacher would say "sticking things together").
- B: Same flag on the plural/singular switch, because the lesson teaches "It's used for". "a pair of scissors" takes "it" and keeps the target sentence. Riko's "They're used for cutting paper." in the conversation is correct, so I kept it.
- C: Reads well. "Snip, snip!" and "Twist your wrist!" are easy to say.
- Decision: teacherScript "These are scissors. What are they used for?" -> "This is a pair of scissors. What's it used for?" (objective), and "sticking things" -> "sticking things together" (A+B). Both are in one patch.

#### L543 How Do You Use It?
- A: The gumball-machine scene is natural. In seg11, "Now, then, turn the handle." stacks two order words. In seg19, "Boop, boop, brilliant!" uses British praise (lowercase, so the block-wide fix missed it). "Push this button. Then, turn it." could mean turning the button, but it is a learning item, so it is a single flag and kept.
- B: The starter has a 14-word sentence (lint warn). In seg9, "Ree, again!" does not say that re means again. The JA aside 動作の ことば in the teacherScript uses kanji, but seg8 says うごきの ことば (single flag, kept).
- C: "Now, then," stumbles. "Ree, again!" sounds like "say ree again". "Hmmm!" for a humming machine may be read as thinking, but only C flags it, so I kept it.
- Decision: starter split -> "I have a new music box. But I don't know how to use it." (B+C). seg9 "Ree, again!" -> "Ree means again!" (A+B+C). seg11 -> "Then, turn the handle." and JA つぎは、それから -> それから (A+B+C). seg19 "brilliant" -> "bingo", JA すばらしい -> だいせいかい (A+C).

#### L544 Like This?
- A: The fixing-the-robot scene is natural ("Not quite. Turn it the other way." is what a teacher says). In the teacherScript, "your friend can say, not quite." puts the quoted phrase in lowercase. The ending "show me how to clap three times. Like this?" is a little unclear about who asks (single flag, kept).
- B: The two meanings of right are taught clearly. In seg9, "Ree, one more time." sounds like an instruction to repeat. seg1 subtitleEn "/ Not quite." vs say "Or not quite." follows the block-wide subtitle style, so I kept it.
- C: In seg9, "Wind, rewind." will be read with the air word /wɪnd/ for "wind". This is a heteronym trap, and it is the opposite of the lesson's model "ree-wined".
- Decision: teacherScript "say, not quite." -> "say, Not quite." (objective). seg9 -> "Ree means one more time. ... Fold, refold. And rewind. It sounds like ree-wined." JA "wind → rewind" -> "そして rewind" (B+C).

#### L545 Tool Quiz
- A: The quiz show is natural ("Haru buzzes in.", "Here's another hint."). At the end of the teacherScript, "Think of a tool and give me a hint. It's used for what?" sounds like Ms. Emma is asking the question herself. In the starter, "Then you give me a quiz!" is a bit flat, but it is clear (single flag, kept).
- B: Is it used for ~? with Yes, it is / No, it isn't is clear. The starter hint "cutting paper" leads to scissors, which are plural, but "What is it?" is normal in a kids' quiz (kept).
- C: seg9 has the same "Wind, rewind." TTS trap as L544.
- Decision: teacherScript "It's used for what?" -> "It's used for... Tell me!" (A+B). seg9 "Wind, rewind." -> "And rewind." and the JA to match (B+C).

#### L546 Let's Make a Paper Plane
- A: The paper-plane scene is natural ("Whoosh!", "It's flying to the window!"). In the teacherScript, "How do you make a smoothie? First, put fruit in the mixer." is wrong for Americans, who use a blender for smoothies. In seg19, "You can't throw flat paper." is not true: you can throw it, but it won't fly.
- B: Same flag on seg19, because the JA says たいらな かみは とばない (it doesn't fly). First / Then is clear, and the -er tools rule in the tip says おおい (often), which is correct.
- C: Reads well. "Er, er, er!" is fine for /ər/.
- Decision: teacherScript smoothie -> "How do you make a cake? First, put eggs and flour in the mixer." (A+B). seg19 -> "Flat paper can't fly." (A+B).

#### L547 Next, Cut Along the Line
- A: The paper-robot scene and "That's OK. Cut slowly." are natural. seg0 "Shall we help?" is British and formal. In seg12, "Press your hands." needs "together". At the end of the teacherScript, "Then, staple the papers with a stapler. Glue sticks are easy to use." comes out of nowhere. seg10 "cutter" is a カッター calque for a box cutter, but it is the phonics word (single flag, kept).
- B: In seg9, "Er at the end means a tool." is too strong (teacher, water), so it should be "can mean". In seg8 and seg20, "First, next, Then" mixes cases because of the block-wide lowercase rule. The JA says First、Next、Then.
- C: In seg2, "Draw it from here, to there." has a stray comma that breaks the phrase.
- Decision: teacherScript ending -> "A mistake? Use an eraser. Lots of papers? Use a stapler." (A+B). seg0 -> "Can we help her?" (A+B). seg2 comma removed (A+C). seg8 and seg20 -> "First, Next, Then" (objective). seg9 -> "can mean a tool" with JA どうぐの しるしに なる ことが あるよ (objective). seg12 -> "Press your hands together." (A+B).

#### L548 Finally, It Works!
- A: The karakuri tea doll is a lovely scene. In the starter, "What do we do finally?" is not natural. In seg17, "Say, Finally, it works!" after "A puzzle? A drawing?" does not fit, because a drawing doesn't "work". "Tea. A warm green drink" assumes green tea, which fits the karakuri context (kept).
- B: The two meanings of finally (さいごに / やっと) are explained well. seg8 has the same mixed case "First, next, Then". In seg12, "Three, two, one, Let go!" has a capital mid-sentence.
- C: In seg10, "El, e, va, tor." will be read as letter names plus "vah". In seg3, "Push it down and, boing!" has the comma in the wrong place. The learning items "Wind it up." and "Wind it up and let go." carry the same heteronym risk as L544 (see the block-wide notes, not changed).
- Decision: starter -> "What's the last step?" (A+B). seg3 -> "Push it down, and boing!" (A+C). seg8 -> "First, Next, Then, and Finally" (objective). seg10 -> "El-e-va-tor." and the JA to match (B+C). seg12 -> "let go!" (objective). seg17 -> "Think of something you made or fixed after trying hard. A kite? A toy car?" and the JA to match (A+B).

#### L549 Let's Try Again
- A: The growth-mindset scene is warm ("Every inventor makes mistakes.", "Let's remake it together, Haru."). In the teacherScript, "we ask, what went wrong?" has the quoted question in lowercase. The ending of seg8, "No problem at all.", is a little flat (single flag, kept).
- B: It didn't work / What went wrong? / Let's try again is a good set. -er vs -or with one sound is clear. Lint gives info: 4 "Let's" in the lesson. Most of them are the target "Let's try again / Let's remake it", so they are kept.
- C: Reads well. "Vroom! Uh-oh." is easy to say.
- Decision: teacherScript "what went wrong?" -> "What went wrong?" (objective).

#### L550 How-To Show
- A: The show format is natural ("Who's first?", "Thank you for watching!"). In the pinwheel steps, "Next, cut the corners." means cutting the corners off. A pinwheel needs a slit at each corner, and the JA already says かどに きりこみを いれます. In seg16, "You're on the stage." would be said as "You're on stage." In the teacherScript, "say, thank you for watching!" is lowercase.
- B: The four order words are the point of the lesson, but seg1, seg17 (quiz) and seg18 show them as "First, next, Then, finally". In the quiz this is confusing because the casing looks like part of the answer. In seg6, Riko is quoted as saying "to the middle", but her line says "to the center". In seg10, the TPR command "Fold the corners to the center." is also said with "middle", even though center is the vocab word.
- C: The starter "Please tell us the steps." is fine from a child host (kept). "Whoooo!" reads well.
- Decision: "cut the corners" -> "make a cut at each corner" in conversation.lines[2], seg5 say/subtitleEn/slide.items[2] and the teacherScript (A+B). teacherScript "thank you for watching!" capitalized (objective, same patch). seg1, seg17 and seg18 order words -> "First, Next, Then, Finally" (objective). seg6 and seg10 "middle" -> "center" (objective, misquotes). seg16 -> "You're on stage." (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Lowercase-after-order-word rule misfires on quoted order words**: when the order words themselves are being taught ("First, Next, Then, Finally"), the block-wide fix lowercased the word after "First," and left the others capitalized. I fixed L547 seg8/seg20, L548 seg8, and L550 seg1/seg17/seg18. **Please scan all 60 lessons for `First, next` and `Then, finally`** (and `Next, then`, `Then, after that`) in quiz options and "you can use First, ..." sentences. Plain sentences like "First, fold the paper" are correct as they are.
- **"Wind" heteronym**: "Wind, rewind." (L544, L545) was fixed. The learning items "Wind it up." and "Wind it up and let go." (L548 tpr[1], expressions[2], L549 conversation) cannot be changed here. Please check how the TTS reads "Wind it up" (it should be /waɪnd/). If it is wrong, a TTS pronunciation hint is better than changing the item.
- **Lowercase "brilliant"** (L543 seg19) slipped past the British-praise fix, which apparently matched only "Brilliant". Grep the block for a lowercase `brilliant` and `lovely`.
- **"Ree, ..." fragments** (L543 seg9 "Ree, again!", L544 seg9 "Ree, one more time."): these sound like "say ree again". If other groups explain re- the same way, use "Ree means again."
- **Syllable spell-outs**: L548 seg10 "El, e, va, tor" -> "El-e-va-tor" (hyphen beat form, as the brief asks). Check other clap-the-beats lines.
- **くるま for "wheel"** (L541): Japanese kids read くるま as "car". Check other worlds for "wheel" translated as くるま.
- Kept on purpose: L541 note "Please use my tools..." (written note, lint info); L543 "Push this button. Then, turn it." (learning item, slight "it" ambiguity); L543 seg0 "Hmmm!"; L544 seg1/L545 seg8 "/" in subtitleEn (block-wide subtitle style); L544 teacherScript ending "Like this?"; L545 starter "What is it?" for a cutting-paper hint and "Then you give me a quiz!"; L547 "cutter" (phonics word, カッター sense); L548 "Tea. A warm green drink"; L549 4 x "Let's" (target phrases); L549 seg8 "No problem at all."; L550 starter "Please tell us the steps."

### Run 9 review notes: L551-560 (world 19, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, OK, US praise, "I say", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First, / After that, / Finally,", AI starter partner names). There are 40 patches in 10 lessons. Every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The English in this unit (must / have to / should, game talk) is clean, so the fixes are small. Themes: (1) "And now, Smile!"-type capitals after a comma before a plain TPR command, which the block-wide lowercase pass did not catch (L553, L554, L557, L559); (2) AI-conversation starters that don't fit the child partner (L551 Riko "I'm new at this workshop", L552 Birimo's "little brother", L554 Riko announcing "Today I'm the workshop master"); (3) quoted model sentences in the teacherScript written in lowercase ("you can hear, sorry", "Haru says, that's not fair", "ask, whose turn is it?", "ask, any questions?"); (4) TTS traps in sound teaching ("g, g" read as "jee jee", "thum b", "Liq-uid. Eq-ual." splitting the qu); (5) "a paper" as a countable noun (L555 poster, L557 card); (6) the two over-12-word lines in L557 (starter, lines[3]). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. All lessons are 206-226 s long with 97-98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L551 You Have to Wear Goggles
- A: The safety-class scene is natural ("Rule one. You have to wear goggles.", Birimo's "I don't have hair!"). seg1 "Tell the workshop rules." and seg20 "You can tell rules with have to" use "tell" without a listener, which a teacher would not say. The AI starter has Riko say "I'm new at this workshop", but she was in the safety class in this very lesson.
- B: have to for rules and "Do I have to ~? - Yes, you do." are clear, and the "Yes, I am" distractor is good. Same flag on "tell rules" (calque of きまりを つたえる). The starter's last sentence was long; "What do I have to do first?" is enough. The teacherScript fragment "Wrench, knob." is odd but harmless (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well. "Shh, the W and the K are sleeping!" is fun to say.
- Decision: starter -> "Hi! I'm Riko. Let's pretend I'm new here. What do I have to do first?" (A+B). seg1 -> "Tell your friends the workshop rules." and seg20 "tell rules" -> "say rules" (A+B). The JA is unchanged.

#### L552 You Must Not Touch It
- A: The hot-machine scene is natural ("Stop! You must not touch it. It's hot.", "Walk, Birimo!"). In the starter, Birimo talks about "My little brother", which the story never gives him. seg17 already says "A little friend wants to touch a hot iron. Tell him!". Kiko's "It says, danger, hot!" reads the sign text as a flat list.
- B: must not vs must is clear, and the safety line "Always ask a grown-up" is good. seg8 "Must is a strong yes, you need to." is a little loose, but it pairs with "a strong no" (single flag, kept). In the teacherScript, "Thumb, not thum b." uses a non-word to show the silent b.
- C: TTS will read "thum b" as "thum bee". "danger, hot" has no stress, so it does not sound like a warning.
- Decision: starter "My little brother" -> "A little boy" (A+B). teacherScript -> "Thumb. Don't say the b." (A+C). conversation lines[3] -> "It says, Danger! Hot!" (A+C). The line is not on a slide.

#### L553 You Don't Have to Hurry
- A: Kiko's slow painting and the "Handmade things are special" line are warm. In the teacherScript, "She is very worried. Is it OK? I'm so slow!" gives Kiko's words no speaker, so Ms. Emma seems to call herself slow.
- B: don't have to vs must not is clearly marked in the JA. In seg9, "It just makes the G strong, g, g." says "strong" where the JA says かたく (hard). The teacherScript also has "Don't have to means, it's not necessary." with a stray comma. seg12 "And now, Smile!" has a capital mid-sentence before a plain command.
- C: "g, g" will be read as the letter name "jee, jee", which is the soft sound and the opposite of the lesson. The missing speaker in the teacherScript breaks the listening.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "She says, I'm so slow!" and the comma removed (A+B+C). seg9 -> "It just makes the G hard, like in go." (B+C). seg12 "Smile!" -> "smile!" (objective).

#### L554 May I Use This?
- A: The borrow-a-hammer scene is natural ("Be careful with your thumb!", "Sorry, I'm using it now. You can use it after me."). In the teacherScript, "you can hear, sorry, I'm using it now" is not how a teacher says it ("you might hear"). In the starter, Riko says "Today I'm the workshop master", which a child would not just announce. That is the exact example in the brief.
- B: May I as the polite form of Can I is clear, and Riko's "Haru, may I...?" to a friend is fine for practice. The quoted "sorry" should start with a capital like every other model sentence. seg12 "And now, Blow the whistle!" has the same mid-sentence capital as L553.
- C: "Say sul." in seg9 is an odd spelling, but it is short and TTS reads it close to /səl/ (single flag, kept). "Whis-tle, not whis-t-le." reads fine.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "you might hear, Sorry, I'm using it now." (A+B). starter -> "Hi! I'm Riko. Let's pretend I'm the workshop master. These are my tools. Do you need anything?" (A+B). seg12 "Blow" -> "blow" (objective).

#### L555 Workshop Rules Poster
- A: The poster scene is natural ("Rule number one: We must wear goggles.", "I'll draw goggles and a big broom."). seg3 "A big paper on the wall" uses paper as a countable noun (same as L484). "We must what?" at the end of the teacherScript is a normal teacher prompt (kept).
- B: must / have to as rules and should as advice is a clear review, and the quiz checks it. The silent-letter review is good.
- C: Reads well. "busy, busy, busy" and "Press, press, press!" are easy to say.
- Decision: seg3 say/subtitleEn -> "A big sheet of paper on the wall, with big letters and pictures." (objective).

#### L556 You Should Rest
- A: The sleepy-Kiko scene and Birimo's whisper are warm and natural. The teacherScript says "It means, it's a good idea." and "Shouldn't means, it's not a good idea.", each with a stray comma.
- B: should / shouldn't for advice is clear, and the "work all night" distractor is funny. seg1 "And, You shouldn't work too late." uses the quoted-sentence capital (product convention, kept). "kw" as a sound label is used the same way in L556-L560 (block-wide note).
- C: The commas after "means" make TTS pause in the middle of the phrase (same as the L488 fix). "A quiet queen takes a quick quiz!" is an intended tongue-twister.
- Decision: both stray commas removed in the teacherScript (A+C).

#### L557 Game Rules
- A: The board-game scene is natural ("Our board game is ready! Let me explain the rules.", "Quack, quack! I love this game already!"). In lines[3], "you have to answer a question card" is wrong: you answer the question, not the card. The teacherScript already says "pick a question card", and seg12 says "a duck space".
- B: First / have to, Then / can, must not is a clear order. lines[3] (13 words) and the starter ("I want to play your favorite game, but I don't know the rules.", 13 words) are over the Stage 2 limit. seg4 "A small, flat paper" is countable paper again. seg12 "And now, Quack like a duck!" has the mid-sentence capital.
- C: lines[3] is long and stumbles on "land on a duck, you have to answer". The rest reads well.
- Decision: lines[3], seg6 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa and seg6 slide -> "That's right. On a duck space, you have to pick a question card." with JA あひるの マスでは、しつもんカードを ひかなければ ならない。(A+B+C). starter split -> "I want to play your favorite game. But I don't know the rules." (B+C). seg4 -> "A small, flat piece of paper with..." (objective). seg12 "Quack" -> "quack" (objective).

#### L558 It's Your Turn
- A: The game-play talk is natural ("Here I go... Three!", "Aww. OK. Birimo, it's your turn!"). In the teacherScript, "When you forget, ask, whose turn is it?" should be "If you forget", and the quoted question needs a capital.
- B: Whose turn / your turn / spaces is clear. Using "square" both as a shape word and as a game space ("a squid square") is fine because the lesson also teaches "space". "Good games are fun when everyone waits for their turn." is a little circular but harmless (single flag, kept).
- C: "S, q u. Squ, squ." is odd to read, but it is short and keeps the letters (single flag, kept). "A squid on a square! Squish!" is fun to say.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "If you forget, ask, Whose turn is it?" (A+B).

#### L559 That's Not Fair!
- A: The argument-and-make-up scene is natural, and the "It's OK. It's just a game." ending is good. Riko's rule line "You can move the number on the dice." is not how a rule is said. In the teacherScript, the quoted lines "that's not fair!" and "sorry. You're right." start with lowercase letters.
- B: The calm-voice message and the "say it calmly" safety line are good (the "calmly" line is tested, kept). The beat split "Liq-uid. Eq-ual." cuts the qu apart, but the lesson point is that q u stays together in the middle of a word. Haru's starter (taking three cards on purpose and laughing) fits seg17 (kept).
- C: TTS will read "Eq-ual" as "ek-yool". seg12 "And now, Squeak like a mouse!" has the mid-sentence capital.
- Decision: lines[4], seg7 say/subtitleEn and seg7 slide -> "You move as many spaces as you roll." The JA サイコロの かずだけ すすめる already matches (A+B). teacherScript capitals fixed and "Liq-uid. Eq-ual." -> "Li-quid. E-qual." (B+C). seg12 "Squeak" -> "squeak" (objective).

#### L560 Our Original Game
- A: The game-show presentation is natural ("Our game is called Star Quest.", "Any questions?", "Can I play it after the show?"). In the teacherScript, "Then, ask, any questions?" has the quoted question in lowercase.
- B: The unit wrap-up (have to, don't have to, how to win) is clear, and the quiz checks the content. The "なまえは いわなくて OK" line is a keeper.
- C: Reads well. "Squeeze your star!" is easy to say.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Then, ask, Any questions?" (objective).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"And now, Smile!" / "And now, Blow the whistle!"**: a capital after "And now," before a plain TPR command, fixed here in L553, L554, L557 and L559 seg12. The block-wide lowercase pass covered "Now," but not "And now,". Scan L561-600 for `And now, [A-Z]` (quoted model sentences keep the capital).
- **"a paper" as a countable noun** (L555 poster, L557 card, and L484 earlier): scan for "a big paper", "a small paper", "flat paper".
- **AI starters that make the child the master or the newcomer**: framed as pretend play here ("Let's pretend I'm..."). Watch for similar role lines in L561-600.
- **Lowercase quoted sentences in the teacherScript** ("say, sorry", "ask, whose turn", "ask, any questions?"): the teacherScripts are less consistent than the segments. A regex such as `(say|says|ask|asks|hear), [a-z]` finds them.
- **Sound labels "kw" / "skw" / "sul"** (L554, L556-L560): odd spellings, but they are used the same way every time and are short, so they are kept. If TTS misreads them, change them all together (for example, "the kw sound, like in quick").
- **Beat splits must follow the spelling unit being taught** (L559 "Li-quid. E-qual."): check other hyphen beats so they don't split digraphs.
- Kept on purpose: L551 teacherScript "Wrench, knob."; L552 seg8 "Must is a strong yes"; L554 seg9 "Say sul."; L555 "We must what?"; L556 seg1 "And, You shouldn't..."; L558 "S, q u. Squ, squ."; L559 Haru's deliberate cheating in the starter; all safety lines.

### Run 9 review notes: L561-570 (world 19, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, OK, US praise, "I say / I ask", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First, / After that, / Finally,", "Last time, we ..." in L562, AI starters with the child partner). There are 31 patches in 9 lessons. L567 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The English in this world is already clean, so the fixes are small. Themes: (1) Japanese that no longer matches the English after the block-wide fixes, or that mixes two words for one thing (L562 きのう vs "Last time", L570 ハンドル vs とって); (2) punctuation around quoted sentences (L565 "ask, any questions?", "The audience asks Is it heavy.", L568 "The title is, how to make..."); (3) lines that are logically odd or out of place (L564 "The inventor thought about the Earth, too.", L569 "Let's clean up... What first? / First, we have to clean up.", L569 "And then, I'll help you.", L570 "Next, ... Next is World 20"); (4) small usage errors ("a paper" for a sheet, "after the craft time", "What a fair!", "Please look", "By pushing means, how"). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 210-225 s long with 98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L561 Great Inventions
- A: The gallery scene is natural ("Look! This is a very old telephone.", "What about this round thing?"). In the teacherScript, "Please look with your eyes, not your hands." sounds like a scolding from a teacher to a child. seg17 "The something helps people something." is an odd way to say a pattern aloud, but it is the product's spoken placeholder (single flag, kept).
- B: helps people + verb is clear, with good recycling (talk, carry, go far, remember). The ou /aʊ/ vs /ʌ/ contrast is correct. Same flag on "Please look" (style guide 4). seg19 "What a bright idea, just like you!" is a little loose but warm (kept).
- C: Reads well. "Ring, ring! Hello, Grandma!" and "Shh, this is a special room." are easy to voice.
- Decision: teacherScript "Please look with your eyes, not your hands." -> "Remember, look with your eyes, not your hands." (A+B).

#### L562 My Dream Machine
- A: Natural. "Dream big!", "Anything goes!", "No more messy room!" are what a teacher says. Birimo's "fly around every country in one day" would usually be "fly around the world", but country is a phonics word and it is fine from Birimo (single flag, kept).
- B: can for actions, has for parts, also for one more is clear. The three sounds of ou (ow, uh, oo) are summarized well. seg0 EN now says "Last time, we saw great inventions." (block-wide), but subtitleJa still says きのうは.
- C: Reads well. "Beep, boop, beep!" is fun.
- Decision: seg0 subtitleJa きのうは -> このまえは (objective (JA)).

#### L563 By Pushing This Button
- A: The cooking-robot demo and Birimo's "Faster, faster! Whoa, it's too fast!" are natural. In the teacherScript, "By pushing means, how you do it." is not how a teacher explains it. seg20 "...speed up a machine with by" is a little awkward, but it is meta-talk about the word (single flag, kept).
- B: by + -ing for "how" is clear, and the safety line (ask a grown-up first) is good. The ough /ʌf/ vs /uː/ split is correct.
- C: The stray comma in "means, how" makes TTS pause in the wrong place.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "By pushing tells how you do it." (A+B+C).

#### L564 Reuse and Remake
- A: The recycling-corner scene is warm ("I thought it was trash. But it's treasure!"). In the teacherScript practice list, "The inventor thought about the Earth, too." comes from nowhere. It was only put there to use "thought".
- B: Same flag. reuse / remake into / can be is clear, and "reuse this jar as a pencil case" is a good model. The ough /oʊ/ vs /ɔː/ pair is correct.
- C: Reads well. "Twist, twist, pop!" is easy to say.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Reusing things helps the Earth, too." (A+B). The phonics word "thought" is still taught in the phonics part.

#### L565 Invention Fair
- A: The fair presentation is natural, and "It's used for finding lost socks." is a fun model. seg13 "What a fair!" is not a natural exclamation. The starter "Please tell me about it!" is polite but fine from a child (single flag, kept). teacherScript "Thank you for listening!" right before "Now, it's your turn" models the closing line (kept).
- B: The three-step talk (what, used for, how) + "Any questions?" is clear. In the teacherScript, "At the end, ask, any questions?" starts the quoted sentence with a lowercase letter. seg19 "The audience asks Is it heavy." has no comma and no question mark. seg13 JA すてきな フェア has a word the EN does not have.
- C: In seg19, TTS reads "asks Is it heavy." flat, like a statement.
- Decision: teacherScript "ask, any questions?" -> "ask, Any questions?" (objective). seg19 say/subtitleEn -> "The audience asks, Is it heavy?" (objective). seg13 say/subtitleEn "What a fair!" -> "What a great fair!" (A+B).

#### L566 Squeeze, Turn, Pull
- A: The puzzle-box scene is natural ("Hmm, nothing.", "It's open!"). In conversation.lines[6], "There's a paper inside." means a newspaper or a document to an American. Here it is a sheet with a drawing. Ms. Emma's "It says, squeeze the sides first." uses a lowercase quote, but it reads as reported text (single flag, kept).
- B: The imperatives review and "What should we do next?" for being stuck are well chosen, and so is the growth-mindset line "It didn't work? No problem. Try again!". seg9 subtitleJa「スクw」 follows the product's existing「クw」 convention (kept).
- C: "Twist your wrists" is a small tongue-twister, but it is short (single flag, kept). "skw" in seg9 may be read as letters. See block-wide notes.
- Decision: conversation.lines[6].en and seg7 slide.items[2] "There's a paper inside." -> "There's a piece of paper inside." (objective, same fix as L484).

#### L567 It's Broken!
- A: The repair meeting is natural ("I'll turn the knob... Nothing.", "Is one day enough?"). The teacherScript practice ("My bike tire is flat. What should I do? You should pump it up.") is real kid talk.
- B: should = good idea vs have to = must is clear, and the quiz ("Which is stronger?") checks exactly that. seg6 JA みつけなきゃ vs the line's みつけなければ ならない has the same meaning (kept).
- C: Reads well. "Vroom, vroom!" and "Crack! Oh no." are easy to voice.
- Decision: no change.

#### L568 How-To Card
- A: The origami-cup steps are correct and the card language is natural ("Is each step short? Is the order right?"). Kiko's "The title is, how to make a paper cup." puts a comma between "is" and its complement.
- B: Same flag. The four sequence words are clear. phonics.tipJa "stapler（e で おわる → r）" is terse but correct, because it points back to the "erase -> eraser, staple -> stapler" rule taught earlier in world 19 (kept).
- C: "Quick, Q U says kw." may be read as the letters K W (see block-wide notes). The rest reads well.
- Decision: conversation.lines[1].en, seg6 say/subtitleEn, seg6 slide.items[0] -> "The title is How to make a paper cup." (A+B).

#### L569 Gadget Workshop Review
- A: In conversation.lines[0], Ms. Emma says "Let's clean up the workshop. What should we do first?" and Riko answers "First, we have to clean up." That is circular. The starter "after the craft time" has an extra article. In seg19, "And then, I'll help you." sounds like Ms. Emma is offering to help.
- B: Same flag on the circular exchange. The learning sentence can't change, so I changed Ms. Emma's lead-in. must / have to / should / don't have to are explained clearly. "Must is a strong rule. Have to, you need to." is a fine teacher shorthand.
- C: seg19 has no "say", so the listener can't tell that "I'll help you" is the child's line.
- Decision: conversation.lines[0].en and seg6 slide.items[0] -> "It's our last day here. What should we do first?" with JA きょうで さいごね。まず なにを する？ (A+B). starter "after the craft time!" -> "after craft time!" (A+B). seg19 say/subtitleEn -> "And then say, I'll help you." and subtitleJa adds と いおう (A+C).

#### L570 World 19 Grand Review
- A: The show finale and the map teaser for World 20 are natural. seg20 has "Next, talk with Riko! ... Next is World 20, Festival Island!", which repeats Next. The starter "Please tell me about your dream machine." is fine from a child (single flag, kept).
- B: The review covers the world well. The JA for "handle" is mixed. The learning item and the TPR say ハンドル, but the segments 6, 12, 13, 16, 18 and 19 say とって, which is a door or drawer pull, not a crank you turn.
- C: Same flag on the double "Next". seg9 "Q U says kw" (see block-wide notes).
- Decision: segs 6/12/13/16/18/19 subtitleJa とって -> ハンドル (objective (JA)). seg20 say/subtitleEn "Next is World 20, Festival Island!" -> "After that, it's World 20, Festival Island!" with JA つぎは -> そのあとは (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Spoken sound spell-outs "kw" / "skw"** (L566 seg9, L568 seg9, L570 seg9, and other world 19 lessons): TTS may read them as letters ("K W"). The JA「クw」「スクw」 follows the product convention from world 7, so I kept everything in this group. If TTS misreads them, change them all together (for example "says k-w, like in quick").
- **"Please tell me ..." in AI starters** (L565, L570, and others in the block, such as "Please tell us the steps", "Please sit down"): the starters now come from a child partner, and "Please tell me about it!" is polite and natural from a child. Kept. "Please sit down" is fine from a host.
- **JA not updated after block-wide EN fixes**: L562 seg0 still said きのう after "Yesterday" -> "Last time". Check L572 seg0 (the same fix) for きのう.
- **Quoted sentences mid-line**: a quoted question needs a comma before it and its own "?" (L565 seg19). A lowercase start after "ask," was fixed (L565 teacherScript). Reported text like "It says, squeeze the sides first." (L566) is kept.
- **"stapler（e で おわる → r）"** (L568 tipJa): terse but correct, because it refers back to the earlier rule. Kept.
- Kept on purpose: L561 seg17 "The something helps people something."; L561 seg19 "What a bright idea, just like you!"; L562 Birimo "fly around every country"; L563 seg20 "with by"; L565 teacherScript "Thank you for listening!"; L566 "Twist your wrists."; L567 seg6 JA みつけなきゃ; L569 seg8 shorthand ("Have to, you need to."); "Well done!" (L568).

### Run 9 review notes: L571-580 (world 20, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (OK, US praise, "I say", lowercase after "Now, / Then,", L572 seg0 "Last time, we", L578/L580 fall, the "Hi! I'm <partner>." starters). There are 60 patches in 8 lessons. L576 and L578 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The English in this world is already clean, so most fixes are small. Themes: (1) "cher" for the -ture sound. TTS reads it like the name Cher (/ʃɛr/), so I changed it to "chur" everywhere in this group (23 places in 8 lessons; same as the W17 fix in L488/L489). (2) Broken fragments that TTS reads with dead stops ("Please come to, on Saturday. It starts at.", "My favorite festival is, because.", "Picture, not pic-ture-e.", "There is is for now"). (3) Capital letters mid-sentence ("OK, Play", "And, Turn", "Quick, Close", "And at the end, Stand") and quoted text that started lowercase. (4) Small wording problems ("Wave your hand to you", "A big land", "A big paper", "says f"). (5) JA that didn't match the EN (きのうは for "Last time", ことしも for "This year", kanji 島). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 216-230 s long with 98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L571 Drums on the Island
- A: The arrival scene is natural ("Look! I can see the island! It's full of flags!", "Shh... listen."). In Kiko's AI starter, "Close your eyes and listen. What can you hear? What can you see?" asks what the child sees while their eyes are still closed. In seg12, "OK, Play the drums!" has a capital mid-sentence.
- B: There's / I can hear is a clean review, and "The S is quiet" for island is good. In the teacherScript, "Picture, not pic-ture-e." is unclear. The point is that the final E is silent, and the hyphen form doesn't show it. conversation.lines[4].ja writes まつりの島 with kanji, but seg7 subtitleJa writes まつりの しま.
- C: "cher" (teacherScript, seg9, seg10 "Cher, cher, cher!", seg20) will be read like the name Cher. "pic-ture-e" is unreadable aloud.
- Decision: "cher" -> "chur" in the teacherScript, seg9, seg10 and seg20 (B+C). teacherScript "Picture, not pic-ture-e." -> "The E is quiet." (A+C). seg12 "OK, play the drums!" (objective). lines[4].ja まつりの島 -> まつりの しま (objective JA). Starter: add "Now open your eyes." before "What can you see?" (A+B).

#### L572 Would You Like to Come?
- A: The flyer story and "Yes, I'd love to!" / "Sure!" are natural. The teacherScript still says "Yesterday, we arrived on Festival Island." (seg0 was already changed to "Last time"). In seg2, "Wave your hand to you" isn't something a teacher would say. The beckoning gesture is "wave your hand toward you."
- B: Would you like to vs Do you want to is explained well. seg0 subtitleJa still says きのうは, which doesn't match "Last time". In conversation.lines[0] "He asked, would you like..." and lines[2] "It says, dance contest...", the quoted words start lowercase. The product writes quoted model sentences with a capital.
- C: "cher" in the teacherScript and seg10 has the same TTS risk. "Treasure, not trea-cher." should use the same spelling as the fix ("trea-chur").
- Decision: teacherScript "Yesterday," -> "Last time," and cher -> chur, trea-cher -> trea-chur (A+B / B+C). seg0 subtitleJa きのうは -> このまえは (objective JA). Capitalized "Would" in lines[0] and seg6 slide, and "Dance" in lines[2], seg6 say/subtitle/slide (objective). seg2 -> "Wave your hand toward you, like this." (A+B+C). seg10 cher -> chur (B+C).

#### L573 I'd Love To, But...
- A: The kind-refusal pattern and Birimo's ten-bananas joke are natural. "Would you like to clean my room? Ha ha!" is a fun teacher joke. conversation.lines[0] "Haru, please invite Riko." is a teacher setting up a role-play, and that's fine (single flag, kept).
- B: The quiz slide for wrong choice B shows "Ha ha! I'd love to, but I'm full. Maybe next time!", which contains the right answer "Maybe next time!". That makes the quiz ambiguous on screen. Slide items must be whole lesson lines, so the target pattern "I'd love to, but ～." is the clean replacement.
- C: "cher" in the teacherScript ("Cher!") and seg10. "yoor" reads correctly.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg10 cher -> chur (B+C). seg18 slide.items[1] -> "I'd love to, but ～." (A+B).

#### L574 Shall We Dance?
- A: The dance-circle scene and shy-Riko support ("Watch first.") are warm. "Shall we dance?" is rarer in everyday American speech, but it is the 中2 target and is fine as a set phrase (kept). "Clap two times" is normal with kids.
- B: Shall we = Let's as a question is clear, and so is the three-sounds -ure review. In the teacherScript and seg8, "Shall we means, let's do it together." has a stray comma (same as the L488 fix). "Answer, Sure! or, Yes, let's!" starts a sentence with lowercase "or".
- C: The comma after "means" puts a pause in the wrong place. In seg12, "And, Turn around." has a stray comma and a capital. "cher" in the teacherScript and seg9.
- Decision: teacherScript and seg8: comma removed, "Or, Yes, let's!" (A+C). cher -> chur in the teacherScript and seg9 (B+C). seg12 "And turn around." (objective).

#### L575 Festival Invitation
- A: The card-making scene, "Dear friends" and "We hope to see you there!" are what a US class would write. "the fishers" (lines[6]) is less common than "fishermen", but it is a real, transparent word (single flag, kept). The safety line "Never write your home address on the internet." is kept.
- B: The when / what time structure is clear. In seg8, "Please come to, on Saturday. It starts at." gives broken fragments as models.
- C: TTS will stop dead on "come to," and on "starts at." "cher" in the teacherScript and seg9.
- Decision: seg8 -> "Please come to our festival on Saturday. It starts at five o'clock." (A+C). cher -> chur in the teacherScript and seg9 (B+C).

#### L576 Lantern Festival
- A: The night scene, the wishes and "The river looks like a sky full of stars." are lovely and natural. The fire-safety lines ("ask an adult", "An adult lights the candle.") are kept.
- B: People + verb is introduced clearly. ear = /ɪr/ is correct. Haru's "My wish is, I want to see my family soon." is spoken style and fine for the "My wish is ～." target. The quiz options "A, an adult. B, Birimo." use the same lowercase-option style as other lessons (kept).
- C: Reads well. "Now stroke your long beard. Hmm!" is fun to say.
- Decision: no change.

#### L577 Color Festival
- A: The museum-photo scene and the safety twist (close your eyes and mouth first) are natural. In seg11, "Quick, Close your eyes and mouth!" has a capital mid-sentence. seg16 is a Say it! segment where Ms. Emma says only "It looks so fun.", but the slide shows the whole line "It looks so fun. Everyone is smiling and cheering."
- B: Same flag on seg16, because the child sees more than they are asked to say. In seg4, "The G H says f." means the sound, but it will be heard as the letter name. JA さけんでる for "cheering" is loose but acceptable (single flag, kept).
- C: TTS reads "says f" as "says eff".
- Decision: seg4 -> "The G H makes an F sound." (B+C). seg11 "Quick, close..." (objective). seg16 say/subtitleEn -> the full line, and subtitleJa from conversation.lines[5].ja (A+B).

#### L578 Harvest Festival
- A: The harvest table, "Thank you, sun! Thank you, rain!" and the card to the farmers are natural. "A deer appeared by the field!" is a little bookish, but it carries the phonics word appear (kept). Hands together and a bow are fine for Japanese learners.
- B: dear/deer as same sound, different spelling is well taught ("Dear is for letters, like Dear Grandma."). The fall wording is already fixed block-wide.
- C: Reads well. "Oof, it's heavy!" is easy to say.
- Decision: no change.

#### L579 Winter Lights
- A: The snowy-town photo scene and "It will be cold, so wear a warm coat." are natural. The snow-bears joke works.
- B: There will be vs There is is a good contrast. In the teacherScript, "There is is for now. There will be is for the future." is hard to parse. In conversation.lines[1].ja, ことしも ("this year too") adds information that the EN "This year" doesn't have. expressions[1].ja ひろばには おおきな きが あるよ (present) for "There will be" is natural Japanese (single flag, kept).
- C: "There is is" stumbles aloud. "Brr" (teacherScript) vs "Brrr" (segments) doesn't matter for audio.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Use There is for now. Use There will be for the future." (B+C). lines[1].ja and seg6 subtitleJa ことしも -> ことしは (objective JA).

#### L580 Festivals Around the World Show
- A: The show format and the presenter tips are natural. In seg1, "My favorite festival is, because." is a broken fragment. seg2 "Country. A big land with its own people and flag." and seg3 "Poster. A big paper..." are unnatural. "a big paper" got the same fix as L484.
- B: The review of the block (lanterns, colors, harvest, winter lights) with because is a good wrap-up. In seg12, "And at the end, Stand up and bow." has a capital mid-sentence.
- C: The seg1 fragment makes TTS stop on "is," and "because." "cher" in seg9.
- Decision: seg1 -> "Then give your reason with because." (JA そして because で りゆうを いおう) (A+C). seg2 "A big place with..." (A+B). seg3 "A big sheet of paper with..." (objective). seg9 cher -> chur (B+C). seg12 "stand up and bow" (objective).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"cher" -> "chur" for /tʃər/**: world 20 used "cher" (and "Cher, cher, cher!") where W17 uses "chur". I fixed all 23 instances in L571-580. **L591-600 still has 6 "cher"** (grep `\b[Cc]her\b`). Change them to "chur" in the same way so the block stays consistent. "zher" (/ʒər/) and "yoor" are kept. They are in scripts/english-words.txt and read acceptably.
- **"Yesterday" in teacherScripts**: the central fix changed seg0 in L572, but its teacherScript still said "Yesterday, we arrived..." and seg0 subtitleJa still said きのうは (both fixed here). Check other lessons where seg0 was changed (L562) for the same leftovers in teacherScript / subtitleJa.
- **Capital after a comma before a TPR command** ("OK, Play", "And, Turn", "Quick, Close", "And at the end, Stand"): the central fix only covered Now / Then / First / After that / Finally. Other lead-ins slip through. A grep for `, [A-Z][a-z]+ ` on TPR command text in say/subtitleEn will find more.
- **Fragments with dangling prepositions or conjunctions** ("Please come to, on Saturday.", "It starts at.", "My favorite festival is, because."): these come up when a pattern with ～ is read aloud. Scan for `to, |at\. |is, because\.`.
- **phonics.tipJa labels** "-ure の まとめ:" (L575) and "ふりかえり:" (L580) have an ASCII colon (same note as W17). They were not changed.
- **Quiz slides must be whole lines**: when a wrong option is a fragment of a longer line, the slide can show the right answer by accident (L573). Check other quizzes where the slide item is longer than the spoken option.
- Kept on purpose: "Shall we dance?" (target, 中2 standard); L573 "Haru, please invite Riko."; L575 "the fishers"; L576 "A, an adult." option style; L578 "A deer appeared"; L577 JA さけんでる; L579 expressions[1].ja あるよ; L580 Birimo's "Please tell me about a festival." (a child host, polite).

### Run 9 review notes: L581-590 (world 20, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, OK, US praise, "I say", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,", "..." in titles, AI starters "Hi! I'm <partner>."). There are 50 patches in 9 lessons. L582 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The English in this world is clean and warm, so the fixes are small. Themes: (1) Japanese-style wording ("the kitchen of the hall", "Excuse me" when entering a home, "Think about you", "The fish is first"); (2) explanations that teach only half the rule or that change the story (L588 "right now" next to "every day", "sometimes" instead of "always", "dinner" when the guests had cake); (3) TTS problems in stress spell-outs ("A-BOUT" read with the letter name, "BRA" in "Cel-e-BRA-tion"); (4) British/stiff phrases ("What shall we do?", "Please begin.", "Lovely!" as praise); (5) say/subtitleEn mismatches (L587 seg8, L589 seg8) and three Japanese errors. The one over-12 sentence (L586 lines[5], 13 words) is now 12 words. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 221-227 s long with 98-99% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L581 Welcome to My House
- A: The hosting scene is natural ("Come on in, everyone!", "I feel at home already."). In the teacherScript, "Lovely!" as praise after "Say it with me." is British, and "Guests say, Thank you. Excuse me." is a calque of おじゃまします; an American guest does not say "Excuse me" when walking in. "Thanks for having me" would be natural, but it is the target of L583, so I did not use it. The AI starter has two greetings ("Hi! ... Knock, knock! ... Hello! May I come in?").
- B: "What a lovely house!" and vocab "lovely" are known keepers. Using "Lovely!" as praise in the same lesson blurs the new word. conversation.lines[3].ja says かべの たいこが すてき, but the EN says "I like the drums" (seg7 JA already has すき).
- C: In seg9, "A-BOUT!" will be read with the letter name /eɪ/, which is the opposite of the weak uh the segment teaches. The same segment already says "Uh-bout". In seg6, "I say, My aunt says, make yourself at home." is a little stacked, but it reads OK (single flag, kept).
- Decision: teacherScript "Lovely!" -> "Great!" and "Excuse me." -> "Thank you so much." (A+B). starterEn: drop "Hello!" (A+C). seg9 "A-BOUT!" -> "Uh-BOUT!" (B+C). lines[3].ja すてき -> すき (objective JA).

#### L582 Would You Like Some Tea?
- A: The snack scene is natural ("Snack time!", "My aunt made it.", "I'd like some more cake, please!"). seg20 "Yum, what a snack time!" is a little unusual but works as an exclamation (single flag, kept).
- B: The "to is for inviting, some is for offering" contrast is clear. The point that "No, thank you" is polite too is good. The "Give me cake!" distractor is a deliberate wrong form.
- C: "Ba-NA-na", "Banan-uh. Sof-uh." and "A panda on a sofa, eating a banana!" read well.
- Decision: no change.

#### L583 Thank You for Having Me
- A: The goodbye scene is natural. In the teacherScript, "Thank you for the delicious dinner." does not fit the story; the guests had tea and banana cake. "Thank you for the lovely lemon tea" is fine from a guest.
- B: Same flag on "dinner" (fact). seg0 subtitleJa "おうちへの おじゃまも そろそろ おわり" is unnatural Japanese. seg9 subtitleJa says "lemon、メ ロン じゃなくて レム-ン", which contrasts lemon with メロン; it should be レモン. The starter (Kiko as host saying goodbye) works as role-play.
- C: "LEM-un. MEL-un. SEV-un. GAR-dun." reads well.
- Decision: teacherScript "delicious dinner" -> "delicious cake" (A+B). seg0 subtitleJa -> おばさんの いえに いるのも もうすぐ おわり (objective JA). seg9 subtitleJa メ ロン -> レ モン (objective JA).

#### L584 When I Was Little
- A: Grandpa Toma's story is warm. "We grow little by little" is a nice pun. In seg17, "Think about you." is not natural English.
- B: "When I was little, then the past. Then say, Now I" is clear. "Think about you" needs the reflexive. The teacherScript's "Yesterday, Grandpa Toma ... told us a story" is story time, not a lesson opening, so it is kept.
- C: In seg9, "Pa-RADE. Bal-LOON. To-DAY." shows full vowels while the teacherScript says "Puh-rade. Buh-loon." This is the hyphen beat form the brief asks for, so it is kept (single flag).
- Decision: seg17 "Think about you." -> "Think about yourself." (A+B).

#### L585 Host Family Day
- A: The schedule scene and Birimo's "three bowls of soup" are natural, and the cooking safety tip is good. In seg19, "The fish is first, in the morning." is unclear (the fish is not an event).
- B: Same flag on seg19; the JA さかなは さいしょ is just as unclear. The going to / will split in seg8 is clear. The starter's "Tomorrow is a holiday!" is fine US usage (a special day).
- C: Reads well. "Rise and shine!" and "Scrub, scrub." are easy to say.
- Decision: seg19 -> "We buy fish first, in the morning." with JA さかなを かうのは さいしょ (A+B).

#### L586 If It Rains
- A: The rain-plan scene is natural ("Rain or sun, it will be fun!"). "the kitchen of the hall" is a の-calque; an American says "the hall's kitchen". seg5 "Pop, open!" is not natural.
- B: Same flag on "the kitchen of the hall". It is also the reason lines[5] had 13 words. if + will is explained clearly. The teacherScript's "This week, let's review Stage 2 spellings" uses a time frame and an internal label (block-wide note, kept).
- C: "Pop, open!" reads oddly. "Catch the rain in the kitchen!" is a strange picture but fun, and it is easy to say (kept).
- Decision: "the kitchen of the hall" -> "the hall's kitchen" in lines[5], seg7, seg18, seg19 and the seg19 slide item (A+B). seg5 "Pop, open!" -> "Pop! Open it up!" (A+C).

#### L587 Getting Ready
- A: The costume scene is natural ("Haru, stand still." / "OK! Don't tickle me!"). The scissors safety line comes after the phonics in the teacherScript, which is a little out of place (single flag, kept).
- B: have to / don't have to is clear, and the link back to World 19 is good. seg8 subtitleEn has a sentence that say and subtitleJa don't have ("It's OK not to.").
- C: In seg9, "J, j, j!" will likely be read as the letter name "jay, jay, jay". This is the same pattern as "Ch, ch, ch!" (block-wide note, kept).
- Decision: seg8 subtitleEn: drop "It's OK not to." to match say (objective).

#### L588 Practice, Practice!
- A: The rehearsal scene is natural ("Haru, you're playing too fast!", "It's not a competition. It's a celebration!"). In the teacherScript, "Ms. Emma is counting." is Ms. Emma talking about herself in the third person. In seg19, "Sometimes he steps on his feet" changes Birimo's "I always step on my own feet". The starter's "every day these days" is redundant.
- B: The teacherScript teaches be + ing for now AND for these days, but seg1 ("Say what people are doing right now. We're practicing every day.") and seg8 ("means right now", with "We're practicing every day" on the slide) give only half the rule, and the example contradicts it.
- C: "Cel-e-BRA-tion!" will be read /brɑ/ (like the word bra). "BRAY" gives /breɪ/ and matches JA ブレイ. The starter stumbles on "every day these days".
- Decision: teacherScript "Ms. Emma is counting." -> "I'm counting." (A+B). seg1 and seg8 "right now" -> "now or these days", with JA updated (A+B). seg9 "BRA" -> "BRAY" (B+C). seg19 -> "He steps on his own feet, but that's OK." with JA updated (A+B). starterEn: drop "these days" from the first sentence (A+C).

#### L589 Why Don't We...?
- A: The planning scene and Birimo's banana contest joke are natural. "What shall we do?" (lines[0], seg0) is British/formal. The AI starter already says "What should we do?". seg8 offers "maybe at lunch" as a general kind answer, but it only fits Birimo's joke.
- B: In the teacherScript, "t c h, d g e, t i o n." starts a sentence with a lowercase letter and has no verb (same as L484 last block). seg8 subtitleEn "That's a great idea! / Maybe at lunch." does not match say ("Or maybe at lunch").
- C: In seg7, Riko's "Ha ha! Maybe at lunch, Birimo." has no speaker, so it sounds like Birimo answering himself.
- Decision: lines[0] and seg0 "shall" -> "should" (A+B). teacherScript -> "The spellings are t c h, d g e, and t i o n." (objective). seg7 add "Riko laughs." with JA リコちゃんは わらって (B+C). seg8 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa -> "That's a great idea! Or, Maybe later." (A+B).

#### L590 Our Festival Plan
- A: The presentation is natural ("Our festival is called the Rainbow Festival.", "What a great plan! I want to go!"). "Please begin." (lines[0], seg0) is a stiff please-command from a teacher to kids. Here a teacher says "Go ahead!".
- B: Same flag (style guide please-command). The JA どうぞ already fits "Go ahead!". seg8 "You learned all of these this week!" uses a week time frame that the app can't promise (block-wide note, kept).
- C: Reads well. "Sketch a badge for the invitation!" is easy to say.
- Decision: lines[0] and seg0 "Please begin." -> "Go ahead!" (A+B).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Stress spell-outs with real vowel letters** (L581 "A-BOUT", L588 "Cel-e-BRA-tion", L584 "Pa-RADE / Bal-LOON / To-DAY"): TTS reads a stressed or capital "A" as /eɪ/ and "BRA" as /brɑ/. I fixed the two clear cases ("Uh-BOUT" uses a form already in the line, and "BRAY" is a real word). The L584 forms are kept because they follow the hyphen beat rule. Check other "-tion" stress lines for "BRA" / "NA" style beats.
- **"J, j, j!" / "Ch, ch, ch!"** (L587 seg9, L586 seg9): TTS will likely read the letter names ("jay"). "juh" is not in scripts/english-words.txt ("tuh", "buh", "puh" are). Consider adding "juh"/"chuh" and changing them all together. Not changed here.
- **"this week" / "Stage 2" in kid-facing text** (L586 teacherScript "This week, let's review Stage 2 spellings", L586-L589 phonics.tipJa "ステージ2 の つづり①②③", L590 seg8 "You learned all of these this week!" / こんしゅう): these assume a weekly schedule and show an internal label. Consider "in this world" / "ワールド20 で". Not changed here.
- **"the kitchen of the hall"-type の-calques**: fixed in L586. Look for "the X of the Y" with places elsewhere.
- **Unattributed lines in the narrated "Let's listen" segments**: Ms. Emma's own lines are fine without "I say". Another character's reply with no name is confusing (fixed in L589 seg7).
- **subtitleEn " / " that is not a quiz**: L589 seg8 had "That's a great idea! / Maybe at lunch." while say had "Or". The block-wide "A / B" subtitle fix may have caught non-quiz lines. Worth a quick grep for " / " in subtitleEn where say has "Or".
- Kept on purpose: L581 vocab "lovely" and "What a lovely house!"; L581 seg6 "I say, My aunt says, ..."; L582 "Yum, what a snack time!" and the "Give me cake!" distractor; L583 Kiko as host in the starter; L584 "Yesterday, Grandpa Toma ... told us a story" (story time) and "my aunt's father"; L585 "Tomorrow is a holiday!"; L586 "Catch the rain in the kitchen!"; L587 scissors safety line placement; L590 "Watch from a safe place with a grown-up."

### Run 9 review notes: L591-600 (world 20, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, OK, US praise, "I say", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next,", "すぎた かたち", AI starter partner names). There are 28 patches in 7 lessons. L591, L595 and L598 have no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. This is the Stage 2 review week, so most lines are recycled target sentences and they are already clean. Themes: (1) "cher" for -ture in sound reviews, which TTS reads like the name Cher (L597, L599, L600; the same fix as L488/L489); (2) Ms. Emma repeating a character's "I" line in an answer segment, so it sounds like her own (L592, L596); (3) lines that end on a bare grammar fragment ("the est, and as as", "I can't wait for.", "First, then Next.", "Now, I like."); (4) one AI starter that contradicts the story (L599 Kiko's "concert" tomorrow, when tomorrow is the festival); (5) Japanese fixes ("きみたいに" reads as "like you", rough "いけ！"). The one over-12 line in this group (L599 lines[4]) is split. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 202-222 s long with 97-99% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L591 Stage 2 Review: People
- A: The review of he/she sentences is natural ("Remember Sunrise Harbor?", "Ha ha! You're talking about yourself, Birimo!"). seg8 "For no, use doesn't." would be "For not" from some teachers, but "for no" is common kid talk (single flag, kept).
- B: The 3rd-person -s, doesn't and does are each modeled with a short answer. The silent-letter review (kn, wr, mb) is clear. Kiko's starter makes sense from a child.
- C: Reads well. "Knock, knock!" and "Boom, boom, boom!" are easy to say.
- Decision: no change.

#### L592 Stage 2 Review: Now and Then
- A: The sleeping-Birimo joke works. In seg19, "It's B! Yesterday, I ate five banana pancakes." sounds like Ms. Emma ate them. seg12 "Did you eat? Act it out!" is a little flat but harmless (single flag, kept). seg17 "Tell me about you." is common casual talk (kept).
- B: The am/is/are + ing vs past contrast is clear, and "Ate, not eated!" targets a real error. teacherScript uses 過去形 in Japanese, the same as school.grammar (kept).
- C: In seg19, the listener can't tell who "I" is right after a quiz about Birimo.
- Decision: seg19 say/subtitleEn "Yesterday, I ate" -> "Yesterday, he ate" (A+C).

#### L593 Stage 2 Review: Future
- A: The plans vs guesses lines are natural ("Hi, future stars!", "Dream big!"). Haru's starter plan ("learn to cook new dishes") is different from his drums line, but a child can have two plans (single flag, kept).
- B: going to = plan, will = guess is clear, and the "I going learn" distractor is a known keeper. In the seg11 and seg13 subtitleJa, "きみたいに" in hiragana reads as "like you (kimi)", not "like a tree". tpr[1].ja already says "きのように".
- C: Reads well. "Ring, ring, ing!" is fun.
- Decision: seg11 subtitleJa -> "つぎは きのように せを のばして。" and seg13 subtitleJa -> "きのように のびて！" (objective, JA).

#### L594 Stage 2 Review: Compare
- A: The place comparisons are natural, and so is Birimo's "The bananas were bigger than my hand!". In seg20, "You can compare with more, the est, and as as." is not something a teacher says aloud.
- B: -er / more / the -est / as ~ as are each modeled. "the est" and "as as" in seg20 are fragments, not the forms the child uses.
- C: TTS will read "as as" as a stutter and "the est" as "the E S T" or "the est".
- Decision: seg20 say/subtitleEn -> "You can compare with more than, the biggest, and as fun as." (B+C). The JA keeps the pattern labels, which still match.

#### L595 Stage 2 Review: Rules
- A: The festival rules and the lost-child advice are natural, and the safety line ("Stay in one place") is a tested keeper. "Throw your trash in the bin" is a TPR learning item. Americans say "trash can" more, but "bin" is understood (single flag, see block-wide notes).
- B: have to / must not / should are clearly split into "rule" and "advice". The silent w/b and -ed/-ing review fits.
- C: Reads well. "Gulp, gulp. Ahh!" is easy to say.
- Decision: no change.

#### L596 Memory Album
- A: The album-making scene is warm. The teacherScript praise "Lovely!" is British-style (the block converted it elsewhere). In seg5, "Where's my hat? I forget!" would be "I forgot!" from a child who lost something.
- B: Same flag on seg5. The JA わすれちゃった is past. In seg19, "It's B! I'll never forget the stars..." comes right after a quiz about what Haru will never forget, so the speaker is unclear.
- C: seg9 "U R E in treasure." and "T C H says ch." are short fragments but they read fine (kept). The seg19 speaker issue is the same as in L592.
- Decision: teacherScript "Lovely!" -> "Wonderful!" (A+B). seg5 "I forget!" -> "I forgot!" (A+B). seg19 -> "It's B! Haru says, I'll never forget the stars at the observatory." (A+C).

#### L597 My Five-Sentence Speech
- A: The speech frame and Birimo's all-banana speech are fun. In seg8, "Now, I like. The past, last year." sounds like the transition word "Now," and not like "the present". Haru's starter "Please start with Hello." is a bit formal for a child but OK (single flag, kept).
- B: The present/past/future frame is clear. The JA uses むかし for "last year/the past" in many fields. That is a little off (むかし = long ago), but it is consistent (block-wide note, kept).
- C: seg9 "ture says cher" will be read like the name Cher. seg8 "Now, I like." gets the wrong stress.
- Decision: seg8 say/subtitleEn -> "Here's the point. For now, say I like. For the past, say last year. For the future, say next year." (A+C). seg9 "cher" -> "chur" (B+C).

#### L598 Rehearsal Night
- A: The rehearsal scene is natural ("Too fast! Speak slowly.", "Breathe in... breathe out..."). Haru's run-on line without commas is on purpose (it shows talking too fast). "We are a team!" is fine as emphasis.
- B: The tips start with a verb, which is stated clearly. "It's OK to be nervous" is a good encourager.
- C: seg9 "D G E says j." will be read as "jay", but this letter-name shorthand is common in phonics talk (kept, see block-wide notes). "Hel-lo, ev-ery-one" uses the hyphen beat form correctly.
- Decision: no change.

#### L599 Festival Eve
- A: The festival-eve scene and the map hook are natural. In seg20, "You can say Tomorrow is the big day, and I can't wait for." ends on "for". Kiko's starter says tomorrow is a concert, but in the story tomorrow is the festival.
- B: Same flag on the starter (it contradicts the lesson's own story). conversation.lines[4] "There's a tall tower on it, and many letters are flying around it." has 13 words (over the Stage 2 limit). In the teacherScript, "I can't wait means, I'm so excited." has a stray comma.
- C: seg9 "ture says cher" has the same Cher problem. The stray comma and the final "for." both break the rhythm.
- Decision: lines[4] split into two sentences, with the seg19 slide item updated to match (objective). teacherScript comma removed (A+C). seg9 "cher" -> "chur" (B+C). seg20 -> "...and I can't wait for the festival." (A+C). starterEn -> "Hi! I'm Kiko. Tomorrow is the big day! I can't wait for the festival! Do you have a special day soon? How do you feel?" (A+B).

#### L600 World 20 Grand Festival
- A: The finale is warm, and so is the callback to the World 1 moves. In seg8, "A host says, First, then Next." doesn't read as a sentence.
- B: The Stage 2 summary is clear. "Maybe next time" as a refusal is a good touch. In the seg11 subtitleJa, "いけ！" is a rough command, and tpr[0].ja uses "いって！". The conversation JA "みんなを ほこりに おもうわ" is a little translated-sounding (single flag, kept).
- C: seg9 "Picture, cher." has the Cher problem. In seg8, TTS can't tell where the quoted words are.
- Decision: seg8 say/subtitleEn -> "A host uses First and Next." (B+C). seg9 "cher" -> "chur" (B+C). seg11 subtitleJa "いけ！" -> "いって！" (objective, JA).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"cher" for -ture**: it was still used in the sound reviews of L597, L599 and L600 (fixed to "chur"). Grep all of L541-600 for "says cher" and ", cher.".
- **Answer segments that repeat a character's "I" line** (L592 seg19, L596 seg19): when the quiz asks about a character, Ms. Emma's echo should say "he/she" or start with "X says,". Check the other review-week answer segments.
- **Wrap-ups that end on a bare pattern** ("the est, and as as", "I can't wait for."): finish the model phrase.
- **AI starters**: check that a starter does not contradict the lesson's story (L599 had a concert tomorrow, when tomorrow is the festival).
- **"bin"** (L595 TPR "Throw your trash in the bin."): this is a learning item, so it is not changed. US kids say "trash can". The owner may switch it everywhere if wanted.
- **JA むかし for "the past / last year"** (L597 topic, lines[2], seg6, seg8, seg18-20; L600 seg8): it is consistent, but むかし means long ago. Consider まえ or きょねん when the block is revisited.
- **Letter-name shorthand** ("D G E says j", "T C H says ch"): TTS reads "j" as "jay". This is common in phonics talk, so it is kept.
- **phonics.tipJa labels** ("ステージ2 の つづり④:", "まとめ:", "ふりかえり:"): these are internal-style labels with an ASCII colon (kept, same note as L485).
- Kept on purpose: L591 "For no, use doesn't."; L592 "Did you eat? Act it out!"; L593 Haru's cooking plan; L597 Birimo's "I'm going to like bananas" joke and the "Please start with Hello." starter; L598 Haru's run-on line; L600 "ステージ2 クリア".

| 546 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! Let's make a paper plane today. I'll tell you the steps. First, fold the paper in half. Say it with me. First, fold the paper in half. Great! Then, fold the corners down. Then, fold the wings. Hold it here and throw it gently. Whoosh! さいしょの ステップは First、つぎは Then で いうよ。 Let's practice. How do you make toast? First, put the bread in the toaster. Then, wait. How do you make a smoothie? First, put fruit in the mixer. Then, push the button. Now, phonics. Opener. Mixer. Heater. Toaster. Open, opener. Mix, mixer. Heat, heater. Toast, toaster. Many tools and machines end in e r. First, turn on the heater. Then, wait. It gets warm! Now, tell me how to wash your hands. First, what? Then, what? | Hi, everyone! Let's make a paper plane today. I'll tell you the steps. First, fold the paper in half. Say it with me. First, fold the paper in half. Great! Then, fold the corners down. Then, fold the wings. Hold it here and throw it gently. Whoosh! さいしょの ステップは First、つぎは Then で いうよ。 Let's practice. How do you make toast? First, put the bread in the toaster. Then, wait. How do you make a cake? First, put eggs and flour in the mixer. Then, push the button. Now, phonics. Opener. Mixer. Heater. Toaster. Open, opener. Mix, mixer. Heat, heater. Toast, toaster. Many tools and machines end in e r. First, turn on the heater. Then, wait. It gets warm! Now, tell me how to wash your hands. First, what? Then, what? | A+B | Americans make smoothies in a blender, not a mixer; a cake keeps the phonics word mixer with its real meaning |

| 547 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today we are making a paper robot. Let's follow the steps. First, draw a line with a ruler. Next, cut along the line. Say it with me. Next, cut along the line. Super! Then, glue the two parts together. First, next, then. These words make your steps easy to follow. Next は「つぎに」。First、Next、Then の じゅんばんで いうよ。 Remember, use scissors slowly and carefully. Let's practice. How do you make a sandwich? First, get two pieces of bread. Next, put cheese on one piece. Then, put the other piece on top. Now, phonics. Eraser. Stapler. Cutter. Scanner. Erase, eraser. Staple, stapler. Cut, cutter. Scan, scanner. Look at the spelling changes. Then, staple the papers with a stapler. Glue sticks are easy to use. Now, tell me how you get ready for school. First, next, then! | Hello, everyone! Today we are making a paper robot. Let's follow the steps. First, draw a line with a ruler. Next, cut along the line. Say it with me. Next, cut along the line. Super! Then, glue the two parts together. First, next, then. These words make your steps easy to follow. Next は「つぎに」。First、Next、Then の じゅんばんで いうよ。 Remember, use scissors slowly and carefully. Let's practice. How do you make a sandwich? First, get two pieces of bread. Next, put cheese on one piece. Then, put the other piece on top. Now, phonics. Eraser. Stapler. Cutter. Scanner. Erase, eraser. Staple, stapler. Cut, cutter. Scan, scanner. Look at the spelling changes. A mistake? Use an eraser. Lots of papers? Use a stapler. Now, tell me how you get ready for school. First, next, then! | A+B | 'Then, staple the papers...' and 'Glue sticks are easy to use.' come out of nowhere after phonics; use the -er words in short examples |

| 549 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We made a wind-up car yesterday. Let's try it. Wind it up and let go. Oh no! It didn't work. Let's remake it. Say it with me. It didn't work. Let's remake it. Good! When something goes wrong, we ask, what went wrong? Look. The wheels are too tight. Let's try again. Don't give up! しっぱいしても だいじょうぶ。Let's try again! で もう いちど。 Every inventor makes mistakes. That's how we learn. Let's practice. Your tower fell down. It didn't work. Let's build it again. Your drawing has a mistake. Let's redo it. Now, phonics. Printer. Timer. Tractor. Inventor. Printer and timer end in e r. Tractor and inventor end in o r. Mistakes help us. Now, tell me. | 578 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! In autumn, many countries have a harvest festival. Harvest means picking rice, fruit, and vegetables from the fields. At the festival, we say thank you for the food. Say it with me. We say thank you for the food. Wonderful! Farmers grow rice and vegetables. In Japan, too, there are autumn festivals for the rice harvest. しゅうかくさいは たべものに ありがとうを いう おまつり。 Let's practice. We say thank you to the farmers. We say thank you for the rain and the sun. Now, phonics. Dear. Deer. They sound the same, but the spelling is different. Dear has e a r. Deer has e e r. A deer is an animal. Dear is for letters, like Dear Grandma. Appear. Engineer. Now, tell me. What food do you want to say thank you for? | Hello, everyone! In the fall, many countries have a harvest festival. Harvest means picking rice, fruit, and vegetables from the fields. | 580 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Welcome to the Festivals Around the World Show! Today, you'll introduce a festival. Use this sentence. In autumn, people say thank you for the food. In winter, there will be lights all over the town. Now, phonics review. Picture. Near. Cheer. Bear. Early. Great job! Remember, use because to give a reason. Show your poster, too. When you present, speak slowly and look at your friends. Now, it's your turn. Which festival do you like? Why? | Hello, everyone! Welcome to the Festivals Around the World Show! Today, you'll introduce a festival. Use this sentence. In some countries, people float lanterns at the festival. Say it with me. In some countries, people float lanterns at the festival. Excellent! Then, say why you like it. My favorite festival is the color festival because it looks fun. 「どこで・なにを する・なぜ すき」の じゅんで はなそう。 Let's practice. In some countries, people throw colored powder. In the fall, people say thank you for the food. In winter, there will be lights all over the town. Now, phonics review. Picture. Near. Cheer. Bear. Early. Great job! Remember, use because to give a reason. Show your poster, too. When you present, speak slowly and look at your friends. Now, it's your turn. Which festival do you like? Why? | objective+lint (uk-word) | US English: "fall", not "autumn" (style guide; same as W11-12 L349) |

| 589 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Let's decide the program for our show. When we suggest an idea, we can say, Why don't we sing together? Say it with me. Why don't we sing together? Super! It looks like a question, but it means, let's try this idea. Why don't we ～? は「～しない？」と ていあんする いいかた。 Answer, That's a great idea! Or give another idea. How about a dance at the end? Let's practice. Why don't we play the drums first? Why don't we invite the audience to dance? Listen to all the ideas. Every idea is welcome. Say thank you for every idea. Be kind. Now, phonics review. Scratch. Hedge. Station. Action. t c h, d g e, t i o n. Now, tell me your idea for a class party. Why don't we...? | Hello, everyone! Let's decide the program for our show. When we suggest an idea, we can say, Why don't we sing together? Say it with me. Why don't we sing together? Super! It looks like a question, but it means, let's try this idea. Why don't we ～? は「～しない？」と ていあんする いいかた。 Answer, That's a great idea! Or give another idea. How about a dance at the end? Let's practice. Why don't we play the drums first? Why don't we invite the audience to dance? Listen to all the ideas. Every idea is welcome. Say thank you for every idea. Be kind. Now, phonics review. Scratch. Hedge. Station. Action. | 598 | `teacherScript` | Good evening, everyone! Tonight is rehearsal night. Here are three tips for a great show. Speak slowly. Look at the audience. Say it with me. Speak slowly. Look at the audience. Perfect! Tip number three. Take a deep breath and smile. Are you nervous? It's okay to be nervous. Everyone feels nervous before a show, even teachers. きんちょうしても だいじょうぶ。ゆっくり はなそう。 Let's practice. Speak loudly. Don't speak too fast. Look at your friends' faces. Speak slowly, and smile. If you forget a word, that's okay. Take a breath and try again. Your friends will help you. We are a team! Now, phonics review. Learn. Festival. Bridge. Waving. Now, stand up and say your first sentence. Speak slowly. Look at me. Great job! You're ready for tomorrow. I believe in you! | Good evening, everyone! Tonight is rehearsal night. Here are three tips for a great show. Speak slowly. Look at the audience. Say it with me. Speak slowly. Look at the audience. Perfect! Tip number three. Take a deep breath and smile. Are you nervous? Speak slowly, and smile. If you forget a word, that's OK. Take a breath and try again. Your friends will help you. We are a team! Now, phonics review. Learn. Festival. Bridge. Waving. I believe in you! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |
